# Evidence ledger

Every compatibility claim should be reviewable without trusting an analyst's
memory. Store compact, redistributable summaries in Git; keep full REA output,
snapshots, and target-derived material under ignored `.work/`.

## Record shape

Each investigation record should contain:

- stable record ID and date;
- question being answered;
- artifact path role, byte size, and SHA-256;
- tool and provider versions plus exact command or REA operation;
- observations, with Evidence IDs when REA returns them;
- inferences and their confidence;
- coverage limits and unknowns;
- next smallest query that could resolve each important unknown;
- affected reconstruction obligations.

## Vocabulary

- **Observed:** directly parsed from the identified artifact or captured in a
  controlled run.
- **Derived:** deterministically computed from observations, such as a hash or
  decoded header field.
- **Inferred:** an interpretation supported by observations but not directly
  established by them.
- **Unknown:** not established within the stated search or runtime boundary.

Decompiler pseudocode is an observation produced by a particular analysis
profile. Names, source structure, types, intent, and runtime reachability can
still be inferred or unknown.

| `rea.official-eu.bitmap-loader` | observed | `FUN_00499320` reads Windows BMP header fields directly, accepts 4/8/24-bit input, aligns source rows, walks positive-height images bottom-up, and writes through a locked surface. Its 8-to-8-bit path either copies indices or maps indices 1–255 to the nearest active-palette entry 1–255 by Manhattan RGB distance; index 0 remains 0, exact matches stop the search, and equal-distance ties retain the lower index. Mapping requires the adapter-derived field at `*(PTR_DAT_0050283c + 0x28)` to be zero and flag `0x80` to be clear. Evidence `ev_369eb7072e0370285398eb132c7560351aaebcc7c030e7c234c3af8554dac304`, `ev_b8a2b07dbc450fa42f70b9983eeece49013e8b19e5eaa29b1413daacbd1fd066`, and instruction Evidence `ev_7dd6593ca4c401cb3fdf5e5627585441b800b92433bdf0768a56049b6a5a0b3f`. | This field is distinct from the render context's cached palette beginning at its own `+0x28`. The reconstruction supports the observed 8-bit startup subset and represents the still-unnamed choice explicitly as identity versus nearest-color mapping. Other original conversion/flag paths remain unsupported. Indirect DirectDraw calls are identified from ABI shape, not symbols. |
| `rea.official-eu.bitmap-16bit-source-conversion` | observed | The championship/root loader sends `select.bmp`, R_INFO, the 14 canonical manufacturer-logo requests, and ten groups of 15 numbered preview frames through `FUN_00499320` with flags `0x81`; separate reviewed-corpus metadata identifies the named files used by this slice as 8-bit BMPs with 256-entry embedded palettes. At a 16-bit destination, bit `0x80` suppresses the ordinary active-palette remap, the decoder copies the BMP's embedded BGR entries into a zero-filled RGBX wrapper palette, and every stored source index is expanded through that palette. Display-format discriminator byte `5` packs `((R >> 3) << 10) | ((G >> 3) << 5) | (B >> 3)` (RGB555); every other byte packs `((R >> 3) << 11) | ((G >> 2) << 5) | (B >> 3)` (RGB565). The decoder initially sets the source color-key low and high words to the correspondingly packed embedded-palette entry zero, which need not be numeric zero; later draw helpers can overwrite it, as the Selection Stretch path does with literal `0..0`. At an 8-bit destination the decoder instead retains indices and key `0..0`, leaving displayed colors dependent on the active display palette. Evidence call paths `ev_3d431192309f5947b393aba526b44506e47f847a8e245dc3df740550aa958f13` and `ev_9a26e617a6593a0b9c65ccb7c0da5386041b4e9a6dca74386df329077d0b596b`; decoder `ev_5cde349cec92f4ca7ed830284a6030e50e6c8e060e6ef07b1c6588e9068eddcf`. | Static analysis establishes logical 16-bit words, not native byte order, pitch, alignment, dithering, DirectDraw stretch output, successful driver calls, or final host pixels. The numeric discriminator value has no recovered enum name. Decoder flags alone do not prove a file's source depth, and the general 4-/24-bit branches remain open. The reviewed Chequered palettes equal FCHE in all RGB entries, but that is corpus-specific; R_INFO has no exact external-PAL match and must not be assigned an invented global palette. |

| `rea.official-eu.display-init` | observed | `FUN_00497be0` enumerates adapters/modes, creates DirectDraw through `FUN_00498020`, selects a bounded mode index, stores width/height/depth, and in the 8-bit branch creates primary/back surfaces, creates a 256-entry palette with numeric flags `0x44`, attaches it to both surfaces, and uploads all entries. Windowed mode uses cooperative flags `0x408` and creates a primary plus separate offscreen surface. Fullscreen uses flags `0x451`, calls `SetDisplayMode(width,height,depth,0,0)`, and creates a flipping primary chain with one attached backbuffer. Startup calls the initializer with mode index `2`, flags `1`, and the selected legal/loading palette before decoding and copying the BMP to both destinations. | `rea.official-eu.blit` | observed | `FUN_0049e3e0` chooses fast versus stretch from original rectangle sizes, treats a fully clipped destination as success/no-op, and clips only against destination bounds. Each clipped destination edge shifts the corresponding source edge by the same literal pixel delta, even for stretch. Optional source color key produces observed flags `0x11` for fast or `0x01008000` for stretch; unkeyed flags are `0x10` and `0x01000000`. Evidence `ev_721aab108dc76dc1e6e4e247552dcea7f1fc56b1ad26a5fd9d1e864b6c295ca3` and exact assembly `ev_275158c51689e978e0f1ed161ef525b73bda580338d596d92ea373842634d4c6`. | No source-bound clipping is recovered. DirectDraw method identities are ABI inferences; call arithmetic, flags, dispatch, and success branches are observed statically. |
| `rea.official-eu.championship-selection-stretch` | observed | `FUN_004c0b70` loads `images\\select\\select.bmp` through `FUN_00499320(..., flags=0x81)`. The setup row renderer submits the full source rectangle `(0,0,256,256)` and destination `(base_x-scaled(0x051e), base_y-2, measured_text_width+scaled(0x0f5c)+scaled(0x0a3d), 33)`; at 640×480 this is `(base_x-12, base_y-2, measured_text_width+63, 33)`. It passes key low/high `0/0`, keyed `1`, and wait `1` to the common blitter. Immediately before Stretch, that helper overwrites the surface color key to literal numeric `0..0`, then invokes the destination-surface vtable `+0x14` with numeric flags `0x01008000` and a null effects pointer. This draw-time overwrite is distinct from the decoder's earlier packed-palette-entry-zero key; the reviewed Selection palette entry zero happens to be black, so both values coincide for this artifact. Evidence loader `ev_7e9b6a39c4404864331e2cdfbff8fcf3206ffe4742eec31724d6678a5d239ff2`, caller chain `ev_89df71e30666dcd6a9d69e588dff01c87686e4ed28bb58af621b98adda3c5ba3` and `ev_bd829a8635be12ebbaa009d556bf71ac5fb5e42d132a78513c53af0b37de31e7`, row renderer `ev_163258f9a86b134d96663a37193f377d14642520d72ec342a88798789a459633` plus exact assembly `ev_07185e4821b3d440f610fabada7eadda14c5a50a6e189f387874e1e960cc9672`, and common blitter `ev_77b5cb79e044dcfdcc0d89d8bc9f866b0d890431f5a51b9625b2c899e6bbf082`. | Vtable method names and DirectDraw symbolic flag names are ABI inferences. The executable supplies no filter choice, effects structure, fractional coordinates, sampling loop, rounding rule, or key/filter interaction; rasterization, dithering, and final pixels remain inside the DirectDraw/runtime/driver boundary. Exact pixel parity therefore requires controlled legacy-runtime capture rather than further static analysis. |
| `assets.startup-formats` | observed | All six startup/legal BMPs parse as 640×480, bottom-up, 8-bit `BI_RGB` images with 256 entries and 307,200 normalized indices. All six inspected PAL files are RIFF `PAL ` version `0x0300` with 256 entries. Loading, loaded, and the three non-English legal BMP palettes match their paired external palettes in all 256 RGB entries; English legal matches only 2/256. | Read-only inspection of the profiled local installation using `inspect_startup_art`; no assets are committed. | `impl.startup-formats` | derived | `cmr-formats` provides bounded startup BMP and RIFF-PAL decoding and retains both logical top-down pixels and exact top-down stored DIB rows. `cmr-core::surface` implements explicit identity and recovered nearest-nonzero palette mapping across the complete stored row, exposing `align4(logical_width)` as the compatibility surface width and pitch so retained BMP padding becomes drawable columns. `cmr-core::blit` implements the recovered rectangle expansion, destination clipping, pre-clipping dispatch choice, color-key flags, and no-op decision. `cmr-core::startup_surface` joins reviewed import lookup, decode, selected active palette, and explicit mapping; synthetic tests exercise this path and nonzero padding end to end. | These remain decode/model/planning slices. Zero surface flags are a corpus-backed reconstruction policy, not a recovered assignment. The mapping mode stays explicit until the gating global field is named. No window, DirectDraw call, presentation timing, or 4/24-bit conversion path is implemented. |
| `impl.indexed-display` | derived | `cmr-core::display` defines a platform-neutral 8-bit backend contract and typed ready state. Initialization tests require device/cooperative setup, fullscreen-only display mode selection, the recovered windowed versus flipping-chain topology, one shared palette attached front then back, and palette upload. Startup-copy tests require a full unkeyed visible-front blit followed by a back blit and temporary-source release; either blit may fail without suppressing the other call or release. Mode dimensions select fast versus stretch without assuming 640×480, and invalid depth/extents/source layouts are rejected before backend calls. `cmr-core::startup_display` joins the verified store to that contract: it parses the palette, initializes and reapplies it, then loads the bitmap and performs both copies. A synthetic integration test locks the complete call transcript and proves a failed second palette upload occurs before a deliberately missing bitmap is accessed. | The startup copy deliberately has no separate `present`/`flip` operation: current evidence establishes the visible-primary blit, while steady-frame presentation is a separate recovered path. Initialization failure cleanup and the meaning of startup flag `1` remain unresolved. Persistent resources are backend-owned and released on backend drop. |
| `validation.startup-surface` | observed | A clean V2-profile import of the user-owned corpus loaded both English startup surfaces through `AssetStore`: legal decoded as 640×480 with all 307,200 stored indices changed by nearest-color remapping, while loading decoded as 640×480 with zero changed indices. | Local smoke test only; the temporary imported corpus was removed afterward and no game asset bytes or generated derivatives are retained in Git. The comparison exercises both explicit mapping modes and does not establish which original runtime mode was active. |
| `rea.official-eu.window-loop` | observed | `FUN_0049db00` rejects an existing `Colin McRae Rally` window, creates the game window, hides the cursor, and runs a PeekMessage/GetMessage loop without `TranslateMessage`. `WM_ACTIVATEAPP` dispatch can be suppressed during setup. Inactive or one-shot forced-wait state blocks in `WaitMessage`; otherwise the loop calls the frame tick. The WndProc handles paint, close, erase-background, activation, cursor, four exact system commands, and two palette messages; `WM_CLOSE` is swallowed rather than initiating exit. Activation stores the active flag, restores lost surfaces on activation, and conditionally marks rebuild state on deactivation. Evidence main loop `ev_a09880a02be35b9ab1e6d9f9751a6fbc9297eb1e7b858d2c61538471071a245e`, window creation `ev_0579000d054e606f6f49772afa4d909f86d2b9f30c718901db40a875feb96146`, exact WndProc disassembly `ev_0168e00763161e5ba423d9be8bf9eed1ae321f94e0b6366eefbd9a4de828b83f`, dispatch table `ev_a27b13f380c3f0d7656c260ca8f7bbb42be84b177a62ae37712ed419db67c25c`, and restore path `ev_34e15f90f630a64495fd01e73ec7a1bcad94caccccdacd47791fe715b35efb17`. | Static analysis only. Win32 symbolic message/style names are decoded from observed numeric values. Actual synchronous message order, user-facing quit initiation, and the semantic names of several globals remain unresolved. |
| `rea.official-eu.frame-present` | observed | A completed steady active frame calls `FUN_00498660` once. Fullscreen samples a back-buffer pixel and then invokes the primary surface method inferred as `Flip(NULL, 1)`. Windowed mode resolves the client rectangle/screen origin and calls the DirectDraw method inferred as `WaitForVerticalBlank(1, NULL)`; after a latched failure it polls VGA status port `0x3da` for a blank edge. | `rea.official-eu.championship-car-scene-submit` | observed | A mode-8 car creates one actor per group-0 record. Signed field `+4 == -1` parents the first actor to the selected preview root; otherwise the field selects a previously created actor. Each local 16-word Q16 matrix is copied to the actor, child actors append in source order, and visible traversal is depth-first. Combined row-vector matrices use `child_local * parent_world`: every signed-i64 product is arithmetically shifted by 16, narrowed to its low i32, and wrapping-added; signed i32 components are then scaled by binary64 `2^-16` and stored once as f32. Group-0 values are mesh levels, active level starts at zero, and each visible active mesh submits source-ordered selector batches as numeric `(primitive=4, vertex_type=2, flags=8)`. Selector `-1` requests material and texture zero; nonnegative selector changes bind material then texture through the scene-global cache. The normal frame orders preview callbacks and palette request before the shared depth/scene pass, then clears all car/root visibility latches, commits the pending 8-bit palette, and presents. Evidence loader/hierarchy `ev_78cdaa3880e4d4afe6f25d3addcbb872e57923ffa23688ef98abf456470cad44`, `ev_36d4b7d362524dfdd534fb3b9affa722401fd223908c67c5fccd02dc7a093c03`, and `ev_d3698bb7d0052e4201be00188a24575fe7a413aa814c0b2ecd7ba1f9045f4602`; matrix composition/conversion `ev_80296b33ccdc927d0f4dac0fcdc8961904604876ad4f68a660d68c0dd97c5d06` and `ev_7f39a332b64d2c6c7380273f63bafcd3742608270c327a1842944f5520f0a6c8`; traversal/draw `ev_2a68e1f69ac5211196ed6a3763ad2c6fb66861664e7ead09f9fc92b352f6a1f9`, `ev_4891577f7a75edb8e10dbbd72ce9a2272ff4b034ae908b008b18af34887c6c61`, and `ev_e10a6a6b67069d8d280a1d3b09caef194fb9abf1c2de554666705074dd8a0435`; terminal order `ev_1994060a23851db4b71dd845f82b9b79365127b88d02ef025e1bdde7357fce2f`, `ev_f6bd79271c30e22a0c98f48204c3132046663c8c28c372341e8251a988afb333`, and `ev_f96c7b43e08eb5a3477a4e6a0a75b5858df261dba4b46f7e65b4df0936034b3c`. | Static analysis establishes the hierarchy, arithmetic, numeric submission, and order, not successful driver output. Selecting one run's complete state, selected-`DDPixelFormat` driver `Load` conversion, native packing, dithering, legacy raster precision, and driver final pixels remain unresolved. Reviewed-corpus metadata finds 16–19 actors per car with one preview root and active level zero; it is not a universal format constraint. |

| `rea.official-eu.font` | observed | Frontend startup loads 16 named `.FON` assets into `0x120`-byte runtime objects. Each file starts with a BMP-like fixed-layout 8-bit uncompressed indexed atlas; the font trailer begins at `0x436 + align4(width@0x12) * height@0x16`. A 24-byte header contains a signed glyph count and recovered height/advance/tracking/gap fields, followed by `count * 16`-byte glyph records. The 256-byte lookup initializes to `0xff`; later duplicate codes win. Text is byte-oriented: mapped glyphs advance by width+tracking, missing bytes contribute nothing, space/caret/underscore have special handling, caret forces a line break, and width exactly equal to the viewport takes the wrapping path. Alignment flags use low nibble 2/3 for center/right and high nibble `0x20`/`0x30` for vertical center/bottom. Evidence initialization `ev_8345ff08fd5af08fc7276b09f3e3482520eef6dda6ce76a593c3ddab7ba28e0f`, startup caller `ev_be5c1919ad25e1a439f2629420021568667ee5910fa1ed645c29baf2cf3c2afd`, parser `ev_6c84c9b9f2921e31c8812920e81f78a7e0745d4c188d16e74db52ae07c84df60`, lookup `ev_6bd3bd2a24ac38cc98b86eac7d9a6d9fa4a3683484f48801165ffc1b3ba00966`, measurement `ev_c7cc86d7a4f3e35866a1093a7fffbdac53ae697dabf44b0a60d80022abdacada`, block height `ev_ac66e6170200677174cee6aea2f62ff2c80cfa4b0611c8e4f0a740880038afb6`, wrapping `ev_f4cc3b71cdb2c19c6a9bef1ebe85478585105405a6ba4c5e238cf2d16f79c6b0`, positioning `ev_0a85e069994aed8a2634f757429c132901550238f7033c2f934e99bd867bdccc`, and glyph blit `ev_34bcba196ecad7b61ccde353a4fd0d7c0f848934d620d08f3523c864224cc78b`. | Parser pointer fields and rendering calls are reconstructed as owned offsets/plans, not copied pointer layouts. | `rea.official-eu.alternate-font-render-path` | observed | All 16 frontend fonts are requested with caller argument `0x80`. The direct gate path adds allocation bit 0 and calls the common bitmap decoder with `0x81`; the initialized alternate gate rewrites the request to `0x11`, and `FUN_00499ef0` derives exact decoder flags `0x90`. That flag-`0x10` source path selects an 8-bit destination when device capability field `+0x6c` is nonzero and otherwise selects 16-bit: one 16-bit branch packs ARGB4444 with alpha nibble zero only for exact RGB black and `0xf` otherwise, while the other packs embedded RGB through device-selected component widths/shifts. The load wrapper `0x4958e0(name,rate,bits,stereo)`, guarded by the init flag, builds a PCM `WAVEFORMATEX` (`wFormatTag=1`, `nChannels=1+bool(stereo)`, `nSamplesPerSec=rate`, `wBitsPerSample=bits`, `nBlockAlign=round(nChannels*bits/8)`, `nAvgBytesPerSec=nBlockAlign*rate`), calls `QSWaveMixOpenWave(session,&desc,0,8)`, stores the returned handle at slot `[counter*0x43]`, copies `name` into the slot record (`0x10c` stride), bumps the slot counter `0xa1f848`, and accumulates a size counter `0xa54708`. The play wrapper `0x495a30(slot,channel,flags)`, guarded by the init flag, looks up the slot handle and, when non-null, records the wave-per-channel at `0xa0bd88[channel]` and calls `QSWaveMixPlayEx(session,channel,0,handle,flags,0)`. Evidence: imports `ev_e1b14748b84e7de704e8a25cdbd760665a00feba4c66dce41ca499a252691670`; callers `ev_dacea03282852c9bf3adf11112ec5b6ec676ff108429d02c8be00a51ae682bab`, `ev_0c23c329b299e73d4f96f9c700c71a370951689814f5cf41af09c29ad0725bc0`, `ev_c3e77a79bb8e1335a66fd0881414ad03c8ac391d87d15b42281d8b3bcb524562`; pseudocode `ev_dda2df051e1a984594c225ee6541b61beac95eb75a6302f25cf8e730d6290c33`, `ev_810c278507223b6ac8bf43016effa447043de21c8204c0d6e642e8e7df5c12a1`, `ev_d6575f008b08fd445d0b4929173328bd496dc9737c90f0412f13404dffb2ff29`. | Inferences: `+0x08`=22050 Hz is the mixer output rate, `+0x14`=30 the maximum simultaneous mixing channels, `+0x24` the game hWnd; the load parameter order is (name, sample-rate, bits-per-sample, stereo-flag); wave slots are a fixed-capacity table (~305 records of 268 bytes) keyed by an incrementing counter holding one handle plus the source name. Unknowns: exact `QSWMIXCONFIG` meaning of fields `+0x04` and `+0x18`; the `0`/`8` `OpenWave` arguments and the `PlayEx` `dwFlags`/trailing argument; whether wave payload bytes come from memory or file in this path; loop/stop/completion semantics (`IsChannelDone`/`StopChannel`) and volume/pan/frequency rules (`SetVolume`/`SetPosition`/`SetFrequency`) are not yet traced; the second load (`0x4959f0`) and play (`0x495a80`) wrapper variants are not yet differentiated. WHINE is speed/gear-driven (window `DAT_004f8654/8658/8650`); POPLOOP1-4 (waves 8-11) are RPM-gated one-shots/continuous above `0x1194`; GEAR (wave 13) is a one-shot on gear-change flag `DAT_00829534`. | `impl.indexed-source-store` | derived | `cmr-core::source_store` owns persistent backend sources behind append-only store-specific keys. Upload transactions roll back their successful creates in reverse order when dropped uncommitted, including after a propagated error or unwind; a caught create error may instead be followed by more uploads and a successful commit. Committed sources can be blitted through the store, explicit release clears a slot before destruction, and store teardown destroys every remaining live source once in reverse creation order. Unit tests cover commit, caught optional-style create failure followed by successful continuation, partial failure rollback, unwind cleanup, explicit release, permanent identity exhaustion, foreign/unknown/released keys, backend blit failure, and final teardown. Both persistent font and root-bitmap pools use this owner and coexist without retaining backend borrows. | This is a reconstruction lifetime/safety boundary, not an original symbol or claim about DirectDraw internals. `create_source` failure must publish no live handle and leave the backend usable; `destroy_source` must not panic. |

Its persistent-text seam lays out raw bytes with legacy flags, validates every fatal glyph, source-geometry, and destination invariant before backend effects, privately binds the exact uploaded font key, and preserves ordered recoverable layout and backend diagnostics during execution. Store provenance/liveness remains an execution-time access check at the first submitted glyph. Valid glyphs use exact index-zero keyed plans with recovered destination clipping/no-op behavior. `UploadedFontPool` transactionally uploads and reuses all 16 atlases through the shared source store; backend blit failures remain separate ordered diagnostics, and foreign or released keys are fatal typed access errors. Transactional rollback is reconstruction safety policy. An empty or fully clipped plan performs no store access, so a stale key is unobserved when no blit is submitted. Present resources copy their `u8` index directly; missing selectors use reviewed-car literal index zero. COPY ignores diffuse/specular, so all four gate combinations have identical indexed pixels/depth while core recording tests separately prove the prepared words reach every draw, including selector-cache hits. Unconfigured, unsupported, and mismatched paths reject before terminal effects. Synthetic integration covers resource→missing→resource, exact indexed output, all gate combinations, explicit session propagation, palette commit/presentation, and teardown. | This is a deterministic source-owned oracle, not legacy-driver pixel equivalence. Frontend entry does not select retained state. Index-zero black is established only for the reviewed car palettes. Selection stretch, split/modulate/linear, alpha, quantization, dithering, and legacy-driver parity remain unsupported. |

A separate Type-2 lit preflight consumes already-prepared word-4/word-5 vertices. Its bounded no-alpha canonical-RGB oracle linearly samples retained palette RGB, applies screen-affine Gouraud diffuse MODULATE, and writes unpacked RGB only after complete validation; it never quantizes into the active indexed target or claims native packing. Tests pin selection priority, every render-depth discriminant and CPU-mutation combination, incoherent-profile rejection, public command adaptation, varying-texel filtering distinct from point sampling, modulation, mutation sensitivity, bounded black default handling, and effect-free target/source/profile failures. | This is a comparison candidate before device conversion, not original-compatible alternate output. | `impl.image-comparison-harness` | derived | `cmr-test-support::image_compare` is the differential-verification primitive (E11) for the reconstruction order's "verify behavior with differential fixtures" step. `Rgb888Image` holds a validated `width*height` canonical-RGB buffer; `Tolerance` models a per-channel absolute delta plus an accepted outlier-pixel fraction, with presets `EXACT` (bit-exact), `RGB565_ROUNDTRIP` (per-channel delta 8 - a 5-bit channel widened by bit-replication differs from its 8-bit source by at most 7, so quantization produces no outliers), and `dithered(fraction)`. `compare_rgb888` fails fast on a dimension mismatch, then walks every pixel and reports the max channel delta, the outlier count, the first-outlier coordinate, and the pass/fail verdict. `expand_channel`/`rgb565_to_canonical` reproduce the standard bit-replication widening so a packed 5-6-5 framebuffer can be diffed against an 8-bit oracle. Fixtures (`horizontal_red_ramp`/`checkerboard`/`shifted`) are deterministic source-owned patterns. 9 tests cover count/dimension rejection, exact pass/fail with first-outlier reporting, the quantization round-trip bound, the dithered outlier fraction, channel expansion, 5-6-5 decode, and the empty image. | This is a source-owned comparison metric, not a legacy-driver pixel oracle; it is deliberately decoupled from `cmr-video` so any backend is held to the same metric. Concrete per-subsystem reference captures and the exact tolerance accepted for each render path are produced as that path is converted (none asserted here); RGBA, structural/perceptual metrics, and sub-pixel alignment are out of scope for this primitive. |

| `impl.present-scaling` | derived | `cmr-core::present_scaling` is the backend-neutral presentation-scaling contract (B11): `LogicalSize`/`PhysicalSize`/`Viewport` and the pure `fit_viewport(logical, physical, scaling)` placement math under a `PresentationScaling` policy (`Stretch` fills; `Letterbox` is the largest aspect-preserving fractional fit, centered; `IntegerScale` is the largest aspect-preserving integer fit clamped to >=1, centered). `DEFAULT_SCALING` is `IntegerScale`, matching the `SDL_LOGICAL_PRESENTATION_INTEGER_SCALE` the SDL presenter was already using. The `cmr-platform-sdl3` presenter selects its SDL logical-presentation mode through `logical_presentation(DEFAULT_SCALING)`, so the backend consumes the project-defined policy rather than a hardcoded constant; a later GPU backend sets its viewport from `fit_viewport` and both are checked against the same worked examples. 8 geometry tests (exact-multiple fill, largest-fitting-multiple centering, 1x clamp + negative-origin overflow, fractional letterbox with aspect preserved, stretch, pillarbox vs letterbox orientation, empty frame, default policy) + 1 SDL mapping test. | This is reconstruction policy for the modern backend, not a decompiled original formula - the shipped game flips a logical-resolution back buffer with no window scaling. | `impl.host-lifecycle` | derived | `cmr-core::host_lifecycle::HostLifecycle` composes the recovered host loop (`host_loop`, B09) and window procedure (`window`) into the end-to-end native lifecycle (B12). The `cmr-platform-sdl3` adapter surfaces the events (`HostEvent::FocusGained`/`FocusLost` from SDL `WindowEvent::FocusGained`/`FocusLost`/`Minimized`, `HostEvent::as_lifecycle` bridging quit/focus, `HostEventState::focused`). 7 lifecycle contract tests + 2 SDL adapter tests. | Reconstruction-policy decision for the modern backend: a platform close request drives the loop quit flag because the recovered `WM_CLOSE` is swallowed and does not itself initiate exit. Saves: `SAVE\SAVESLOT` (`0x4f1290`) + `<n>` + `.cms` (`0x4f12a0`), loader `FUN_0044edac` reads 12 slots of exactly `0x6ac` bytes (wrong size -> zeroed/empty), browser `FUN_0044f2f0` shows "EMPTY SLOT" (`0x4f12b0`) for a missing file. | Static analysis of `.work/official-eu/Rally.exe`. | `impl.interactive-native-root-lifecycle` | derived | `cmr-platform-sdl3::advance_root_lifecycle` wires the B12 host lifecycle into the interactive native root-menu loop (D18). Complete-menu navigation beyond the recovered `RootInteraction` states is F-series frontend work. |

| `rea.official-eu.save-slots` | observed | Save-slot/profile persistence (F03). A save is a single `0x6ac`-byte record written whole by `FUN_0044ef50` via `FUN_00496580` (`OpenFile` with `OF_CREATE` 0x1000, then `WriteFile` - truncate+overwrite, no positioning) to `SAVE\SAVESLOT<n>.cms` under the writable base; 12 slots. `FUN_0044ef50(slot)` (called from save menu `FUN_0043bce0` case 7, cursor `DAT_00551b5c`): allocates `0x6ac`, copies `0x1a4` dwords (`0x690`) from `*(DAT_00d3ca80+0x2c)` to record `+0x0c`, stores trailing scalars `record+0x69c`=`*(DAT_00d3ca80+0x20)`, `+0x6a0`=`+0x24`, `+0x6a4`(u16)=`+0x28`, zeroes `0x54` bytes at `+0x1c`, writes magic `0x12348765` at `+0x04` and const `0x690` at `+0x08`, sets `+0x6a6`=`(*(byte*)(DAT_00d3ca80+0x18)>>3)&3`, builds a seed at `+0x00` from three `FUN_004cfac0` RNG words (`((r0<<10)^r1)<<10 ^ r2 | 0xAA000000`) and `+0x6a8`=`~seed`, then writes all `0x6ac` bytes. Validity/occupancy `FUN_0044eed0(slot)` over `DAT_0055d74c + slot*0x6ac`: `+0x04==0x12348765 && +0x08==0x690 && +0x6a8==~(+0x00)`. Loader `FUN_0044ed90` allocates `DAT_0055d74c` (`0x5010`=12x`0x6ac`), per slot reads the whole file (`FUN_00495ed0`), and copies it in only when the size is exactly `0x6ac` (else zeroes the record), then builds flag table `DAT_0055c248` (12 ints) and display-line table `DAT_0055c298` (12 x `0x104`); `FUN_0044f210` computes a 3-char standings tag into `+0x0c`; a `+0x150` sub-record array (stride `0x10`, flag bit `0x10` at `+8`) holds progress. | Static analysis of `.work/official-eu/Rally.exe`. **No delete primitive** (`DeleteFileA`/`_unlink`/`remove` all `name_not_found`; no delete/unlink strings) and **no payload checksum** (the `~seed` complement covers only the 4-byte seed). The validity triple doubles as a format-version gate (const `0x690` = payload length). There is no free-text profile name: identity is the championship snapshot; the `+0x0c` tag is derived, not entered. The slot->runtime load-apply is dispatched indirectly (function pointer) and was not statically pinned; `+0x6a7` is unwritten padding; the `0x690` payload and the `+0x150` sub-record semantics are game data, not decoded. |

Create and save are the same path (no separate create). There is no delete primitive in the original - a portable host deletes by removing the file, which then classifies Empty; `finalize_slot_for_save` stamps the header but the caller supplies the payload and the slot->runtime apply. |

The save-record<->live-struct mapping is `record_offset(live)=live-0x20` and `payload_offset(live)=live-0x2c`, both guarded (`checked_sub` -> `None` below the window). Event-progress decode: `event_started(flag)` (bit `0x10`), `event_completed_stages(flag)` (low nibble), `current_event_index(flags)` (first started-but-incomplete, capped at `PROGRESS_ELEMENT_MAX=16`). Descriptor packing: `pack_descriptor(country,stage,mode)` + `descriptor_country`/`_stage`/`_mode` unpackers and `location_string_id`/`mode_string_id` (bases `0x3c`/`0x2f`). Difficulty selectors: `rally_school_task` (`>>5`), `rally_school_difficulty` (`(>>3)&3`), `mode_tier` (`(>>3)&3`). `CarStatsEntry`/`parse_car_stats_entry` model the `0x0c`-byte stats entry shape (primary/secondary/class_index) without the figures. 7 tests cover the dimension/`car_resources` consistency, the verified offset anchors + below-window guards, the flag decode, the current-event cursor (incl. none-started / all-completed), the descriptor round-trip + field masking + string-id maps, the Rally-School/mode-tier selectors, and the stats-entry parse. | Structure only: country/car/stage names, car codes, BHP/stat figures, difficulty labels, points, and the `.TIM` stage tables are game data and are neither modeled nor vendored - only offsets, strides, bit layouts, and string-id bases are encoded. The event-progress write path, the progress element's `+0x00..+0x07` fields, and the full payload field semantics (F06) are not modeled. `CAR_COUNT` references `car_resources` rather than redefining the car dimension. |

Entrant count = `DAT_00550c08` (or `2` in 2-player). AI opponent times are generated per stage by `FUN_00465d40` from a per-country car-tier table `&DAT_00506e48` (stride `0x14`, indexed by country) scaled by RNG `FUN_004cfac0` between a base and total time - not table-stored. Best/record times: `FUN_004ba4b0(country, stage)` returns a pointer into the mutable data image `PTR_DAT_0050622c` at `(country*7 + stage)*0x1e + 0xffc` (championship) or `stage*0x1e + 0x175e` (alt mode, `+0x166 & 0x40`); stride `0x1e`, 7 stages/country, split bests read as ushorts `+0xc..0x1a`, total at `+0x1a`; `FUN_0044f6d0` OR's `PTR_DAT_0050622c[0x182] |= 0x20` (runtime mutation). Championship predicate `FUN_00485bb0`: `if(+0x166 & 0x40) return (+0x168 & 0xf0) != 0x20; return (+0x166 & 0x20) || (+0x168 & 0xf0) == 0x60`. Opponent tier table, record times, stage names, and the `0x1c`-stride pace/ghost telemetry are external/procedural game data (offset/stride/shape only). |

Records: `championship_record_offset(country, stage)` (`(country*7+stage)*0x1e + 0xffc`) and `alt_record_offset(stage)` (`stage*0x1e + 0x175e`) with `RECORD_STRIDE=0x1e`, `STAGES_PER_RECORD_COUNTRY=7`. Mode decode: `country_index`/`stage_index` over `+0x168`, `game_mode`/`uses_record_path` over `+0x166` (`MODE_ENUM_MASK=0x1c`, `CHAMPIONSHIP_TYPE_FLAG=0x40`, `MODE_SUBFLAG=0x20`, `SUBMODE_RECORD=2`), and the `is_championship`/`is_two_player_head_to_head` predicates. 8 tests cover the slot strides, the flag reset/enter/decode bit ops, time ranking with stable ties + inverse, the weighted championship score + ranking, both record-offset formulas across all countries, the country/stage/sub-mode decode, and the championship/two-player predicate branches. | Structure only: opponent AI time tables, record times, stage names, points (there are none), and the external pace/`.TIM` resources are game data and are neither modeled nor vendored - only offsets, strides, bit layouts, ranking formulas, and the mode predicates are encoded. | `impl.windows-portability` | derived | I04 Windows portability of the compatibility slice. The SDL-free portable crates (`cmr-core`, `cmr-formats`, `cmr-test-support`, `cmr-assets`, `cmr-video`) type-check for the `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` target with all test targets enabled (`cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p cmr-core -p cmr-formats -p cmr-test-support -p cmr-assets -p cmr-video --all-targets`). A workspace audit found no Linux-only source assumptions in production code: filesystem metadata goes through cross-platform `std::fs::symlink_metadata`, and the only platform-gated code is `#[cfg(unix)]` test helpers (symlink creation for the symlink-rejection tests, and a non-UTF-8 runtime-data-path test) which Windows skips. The path-safety rejection shared by `cmr-core::audio_render::FileWaveSource::safe_path` and `cmr-assets::resolver::normalize_request` is Windows-correct by construction (rejects leading `/`/`\`, drive-qualified `C:` names, and `.`/`..`/empty components after `\`->`/` normalization); both reject-tests are hardened with backslash-form traversal, UNC, and drive-relative inputs. | Verification + test hardening milestone; no new reconstructed behavior. `cargo check --target` type-checks without the mingw linker, so this proves source portability, not a linked Windows binary. The SDL host crates (`cmr-platform-sdl3`, `cmr-app`) depend on `sdl3 =0.20.0`, whose native SDL3 library must be provisioned to build/link for Windows - gated like the Linux displayed run (I07), as is a displayed end-to-end run on real Windows. The path-safety rule is now a single shared helper (`cmr_assets::normalize_relative_path`) used by both `cmr-assets::resolver` and `cmr-core::audio_render::FileWaveSource` - cmr-core depends on cmr-assets, so the one rule lives at the lower layer and a hardening applies to both. |

| `impl.ci-workflow` | derived | I05 continuous-integration workflow `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Three jobs on push/PR to `main` (plus `workflow_dispatch`), with `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`, `Swatinem/rust-cache@v2`, and `concurrency` cancel-in-progress. `portable-gate` (ubuntu): `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy <portable-slice> --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test <portable-slice>`. `windows-cross-check` (ubuntu): adds `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` and runs the I04 `cargo check --target … <portable-slice> --all-targets`. `full-workspace` (ubuntu + windows matrix): `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --features cmr-platform-sdl3/bundled-sdl3 -- -D warnings` and `cargo test --workspace --features cmr-platform-sdl3/bundled-sdl3`. The `bundled-sdl3` feature was added to `cmr-platform-sdl3` as a passthrough to `sdl3/build-from-source`, so the SDL jobs build SDL3 from source (CMake + C toolchain only) rather than needing a system library; the default still links system SDL3. | Build/CI infrastructure, no reconstructed behavior. The `portable-gate` and `windows-cross-check` commands are verified locally (all pass) and the `bundled-sdl3` feature is confirmed valid (a control bogus-feature errors; it does not) and resolving to `sdl3/build-from-source` with the native default workspace unchanged. **GitHub Actions is not run in this environment**, so the `full-workspace` SDL source-build jobs (both runner OSes) are authored but not executed here and need a first live run to confirm runner provisioning; lint strictness uses clippy `-- -D warnings` (not a global `RUSTFLAGS`) so dependency builds are not denied. |

| `rea.official-eu.c3d-entry-semantics` | observed | C3D group-1 entry field semantics proven from the draw-submission consumer `FUN_004a2b70` (0x4a2b70, the C3D loader/retained-primitive builder; validates `CM3D` at `0x5027c8`, signature `(record*, param_2 render-class, …, byte flags)`, `param_2` a caller-supplied pass selector 0..10, not on-disk). Group-1 record header: `+0x10` u16 = 32-byte-entry/vertex count, `+0x12` u16 = 24-byte-entry/face count; 32-byte entries at `+0x30`, 24-byte entries at `+0x30 + count32*0x20`; strides 0x20/0x18. **32-byte entry (vertex)**: the loop reads 8 i32 Q16.16 (`piVar12 += 8`), each `*1.5258789e-05`; comps 0-2 -> runtime `+0/+4/+8` position via the affine transform `FUN_0048fa60` (`out[i] = M[0xc+i] + Σ_j M[j*4+i]*v[j]>>16`), comps 3-4 -> runtime `+0x18/+0x1c` texture U/V (draw-time `U>1.0`->`-1.0`, `V+1.0`), comps 5-7 -> runtime `+0x0c/+0x10/+0x14` normal (fed to a fast-inverse-sqrt normalize + per-node max-brightness `local_5c[0x27]`). **24-byte entry (triangle)**: `+2/+4/+6` u16 -> prim `+0x14/+0x16/+0x18` vertex indices; `+8` i16 group-2 ordinal (`>=0` indexes `group2_resources[ord]`, reads its `+2` u16 -> prim `+0x04` texture handle; `-1` -> prim `+0x04 = 0xFFFFFFFF`, flat color via `FUN_004b01d0`); `+0x0a/+0x0e/+0x12` u32 -> prim `+0x08/+0x0c/+0x10` per-vertex colors (transparent class `param_2==2` forces byte 3 of each to `0x96` = alpha; flat default colors otherwise come from per-class `.data` `DAT_0050226c/74/7c/84/8c` when model color `DAT_00b643f8==0`); `+0x16` u16 -> prim `+0x00` header word for classes {0,1}; `+0x00` u16 **never read**. | `rea.official-eu.c3d-view-inverse` | observed | The camera VIEW matrix is the **affine inverse of the camera-world matrix** (`FUN_0048f460`), not a look-at or BAM composition. The matrix is 16 Q16.16 `int` words, column-major: basis columns at word indices `0..=2`/`4..=6`/`8..=10`, translation at `0xc..=0xe`, homogeneous row `0x3`/`0x7`/`0xb = 0`, `0xf = 0x10000`. The routine computes three cofactors `cof0 = m5*m10 - m9*m6`, `cof1 = m9*m2 - m1*m10`, `cof2 = m1*m6 - m5*m2` (each a Q16.16 multiply `(a*b)>>0x10` via a 64-bit intermediate), the determinant `det = cof2*m8 + cof1*m4 + cof0*m0`, and - when `det != 0` - the integer reciprocal `recip = 0x100000000 / det` (an `i64` divide truncated to `i32`). The inverse basis is the adjugate scaled by `recip`: `out0=recip*cof0`, `out1=recip*cof1`, `out2=recip*cof2`, `out4=recip*(m8*m6-m4*m10)`, `out5=recip*(m0*m10-m8*m2)`, `out6=recip*(m4*m2-m0*m6)`, `out8=recip*(m4*m9-m8*m5)`, `out9=recip*(m8*m1-m0*m9)`, `out10=recip*(m0*m5-m4*m1)` (all Q16.16 multiplies). The inverse translation applies the inverse basis to the negated source translation: `out[0xc] = (-m14)*out8 + (-m13)*out4 + (-m12)*out0`, likewise `0xd`/`0xe` over the next basis rows; `out[0xf] = 0x10000`, `out[3]=out[7]=out[0xb]=0`. A singular basis (`det == 0`) calls `FUN_0048ed80`, which writes a Q16.16 identity. The companion transform `FUN_0048fa60` applies a matrix to a point as `out[i] = m[0xc+i] + sum_j ((m[4*j+i]*v[j])>>0x10)`. The per-frame camera setup `FUN_004a7d70` computes this inverse from the camera object's world matrix at `camera+0xd4` into the device context slot `ctx+0x4100`, converts all 16 Q16.16 words to float (`* 1.5258789e-05` = `2^-16`), and calls the device vtable `+0x68` (`SetTransform`) with state `2` = `D3DTRANSFORMSTATE_VIEW`; the projection builder `FUN_004a71b0` likewise uses state `3` and per-node WORLD uses state `1`. **Decisive negative: there is no CPU matrix-matrix concatenation** - WORLD * VIEW * PROJECTION is concatenated by Direct3D per vertex, so a software oracle must perform that concatenation itself. | Static analysis of `.work/official-eu/Rally.exe`. The routine is **pure integer arithmetic** (64-bit multiply reduced by `>>0x10`, one 64-bit reciprocal divide), so it is byte-exact reproducible with no 1990s-driver floating-point dependency - unlike the rasterizer's pixel evaluation. The Q16.16 reciprocal truncates toward zero; exact parity for non-power-of-two determinants follows from the identical integer sequence, not a float tolerance. No camera-spline or per-track transform tables transcribed. |

| `rea.official-eu.c3d-batch-builder` | observed | The batch builder `FUN_004b2340` turns the per-frame runtime primitive list into indexed `D3DLVERTEX` draw batches. The three vertex ordinals (`prim+0x14`/`+0x16`/`+0x18`, `u16`) index the LVERTEX array (stride `0x20`); each is **deduplicated by ordinal** via a first-use linear scan of the batch's ordinal list, appending the `0x20`-byte LVERTEX and a `0xc`-byte side-data record on first use and emitting the local vertex index (3 per triangle) either way. A flushed batch record holds: `[0]` texture handle (from the run's prim), `[1]` index buffer, `[2]` vertex count, `[3]` LVERTEX buffer, `[4]` index count, `[5]` side-data buffer, `[6]` next-batch pointer (`0` at the tail); buffers are allocated through `FUN_004990d0`. | Static analysis of `.work/official-eu/Rally.exe`. The topology (consecutive-run grouping on one selector, ordinal dedup by first use, three local indices per triangle) is exactly what `impl.c3d-batch` already reconstructs; this evidence binds that selector to the texture handle and fixes the runtime-prim field offsets and the batch-record layout. The row-major, row-vector convention matches `cmr-video`'s `decode_matrix`/`multiply_row_vector` (`out[c] = sum_r v[r] * m[4*r+c]`), so the matrices feed the per-vertex transform directly. `viewport_words` reuses the shared `root_menu_c3d_viewport` builder because the D3DVIEWPORT2 clip volume (`{-1, 2, W/H, 2*(W/H), 0, 1}`) is scene-independent - the gameplay and menu viewports are identical. 6 tests: near plane -> NDC z 0 and far plane -> 1 (with `w'` carrying z), the VIEW moving a world point to the view-space origin and preserving a +Z offset, the WORLD translation round-trip, and the viewport words matching the E05 `viewport()` fields plus extent rejection - each replicating the raster's exact row-vector multiply. | Convention bridge and builders only; no scene data vendored. The convention equivalence is checked by replicating `multiply_row_vector` in the test (cmr-core cannot depend on cmr-video); driving the real `transform_vertex` with arbitrary vertices is not possible from a cross-crate test because the resource vertex constructor is test-only within cmr-core. | 

| `impl.c3d-scene-oracle` | derived | `cmr-video` exposes the gameplay-scene oracle's transform entry: `SoftwareC3dTransformState::for_gameplay_scene(width, height, camera_world_q16, node_world_q16)` composes the `cmr_core::c3d_camera` builders (node WORLD, camera VIEW = inverse of the camera-world matrix, baseline PROJECTION, scene-independent viewport) into the transform state the software raster already consumes, and `world`/`view`/`projection`/`viewport` accessors expose it. Rendering a gameplay batch is then `preflight_root_c3d_batch`/`preflight_root_c3d_copy_batch`(command, state) - the existing transform/clip/cull/screen pipeline - with gameplay matrices in place of the fixed menu matrices; no new raster code is required. The `SoftwareC3dPreparedCopyBatch` now exposes `triangles`/`source_triangle_count`/`fully_clipped_source_triangle_count`/`counter_clockwise_triangle_count` for inspecting a prepared batch. **An end-to-end test proves the oracle over the real command path**: a synthetic one-triangle C3D resource goes through the actual asset import, `load_root_menu_c3d`, topology preparation, `materialize_root_menu_mode2_resource_scaled`, and the frame pipeline, then `for_gameplay_scene` matrices drive the preflight - asserting the triangle is seen and visible under a near camera, the result is deterministic, a farther camera yields a different (smaller) projection, and every screen vertex is finite and inside the back buffer. | Composition, the transform entry, and the prepared-batch accessors. The synthetic fixture reproduces the C3D format shape only (no game payload). The visual/differential oracle (exact pixels, multi-object scene-graph traversal, and the opaque/transparent pass orchestration) remains. | 

Hash abbreviations in prose are display-only. Machine-readable manifests must
always retain complete hashes.
